## Plan

Orbit view
1. Check fit overlay, measure Io disc and shadow in the hero.
2. Hero 2x (upscale.py), Io and shadow painted out of the hero pixels (image tool, kept only inside the mask).
3. Unproject, light curve (tools/assets/lightcurve.py), delit hero side.
4. Far side and poles: whole map low pass, then tiles, poles on a polar projection. 8192 x 4096.
5. Planet shader copy in `src/stops/jupiter/`: curve light, Io shadow computed from the Sun direction, band flow (zonal displacement by latitude, zero at hold mid), GRS rotation, flow layer of fine wisps with a soft shadow, limb edge haze on lit and night side.
6. Io: sphere with painted map, hero pixels on the hero side.
7. Sky patch from the hero sky.

Terrain view
- Relief of jupiter-close: 2x, sky cut, depth, extended canvas (aprons left, right, bottom, behind entry), flow map (storm rotation, small vortices, band shear), wisp layer with shadow, seeded lightning, haze cover.

Dive and rise
- Patch levels at most 2x apart from the wrap to the terrain, top down views of the Great Red Spot in the style of the close image, predictor first.


## Measured so far (orbit view)

- Hero geometry: disc centre (1406.6, 789.0) r 706.1 px. Io in hero about (1524, 1000) r 46 px. Its shadow: ellipse centre (1656, 1030), about 190 x 120 px, soft edge, about 1.3 x the size of Io.
- Fit: camera (0.001547, -0.003452, 4.30454), sunDir (-0.744226, 0.139788, 0.653136): Sun 49 deg left of the camera, terminator 41 deg right of centre. Io (0.129142, -0.232241, 1.053324) r 0.049425. Computed shadow centre about (0.273, -0.259, 0.926), ndl there 0.366. Globe quaternion (0.024833, 0.0001797, -4.5e-06, 0.9996916).
- Light fit (`wrap/delight.py`): seen = A(lat) x C(ndl) x L(mu), alternating medians. `src/stops/jupiter/curve.json` (33 knots) and `limb.json` (17 knots over mu).
  - C(ndl), R: 1.0 at 1, 0.56 at 0.75, 0.26 at 0.5. Night floor (0.0045, 0.0106, 0.0223) x albedo: hero night is about (5, 9, 12) in 8 bit, bluish, never black.
  - L(mu), R: 3.47 at mu 0 to 0.12, 2.96, 2.45, 2.03, 1.72, 1.48, 1.29, 1.15, 1.05, 0.99, 0.98, 0.99, 1, 1. G and B lower (2.89, 2.43 at mu 0). So the hero brightens toward the limb. The shader needs a clamp on C x L for other phase angles.
  - A(lat) mean (0.43, 0.32, 0.24). The wrap stores tone coded albedo (sRGB of filmic), the shader decodes with inverseFilmic.
- Equirect (u = lon/360 + 0.5, north at top): hero pixels cover lon -62 to +36, lat +-65. Weight = smoothstep(0.2, 0.42, mu) x smoothstep(0.035, 0.11, curve G). Zonal profile valid for lat -54 to +61. GRS at 4k px (1848, 1142), radii (150, 90): lon -17.5, lat -10.
- Hero poles: north cap is dark grey brown with small cyclones. The stripe guide is flat tan there, so the poles need their own darker mottled paint on a polar projection.
- Rise frame 3296 (stand-in): planet at the right of the frame, seen from the west and south, mostly painted far side and south pole, nearly full phase. The far side must hold up at hero quality.

## Image tool notes

- seedream_5 gives 2816 x 1584, exact 16:9, edits stay pixel aligned. nano_banana_pro gives 2752 x 1536 and stretches content 0.8 percent vertically: to align with a 1792 x 1008 input, resize to 1792 x 1000 and reflect pad 4 px top and bottom. It is sharper and its fills look more natural.
- Io removal: nano_banana_pro take kept (`gen/ioremove_nbp_al.png`), seedream take rejected (bland fill). Kept only inside the mask, low frequencies matched to the hero.
- Io map: seedream take kept (`gen/iomap_sd5.png`), nano_banana_pro take rejected (pattern repeats twice).

## Far side plan

- Tiles at 8192 scale, 2816 x 1584 each (123.75 x 69.6 deg): rows y 512 to 2096 and 2000 to 3584, columns x 4400 to 7216, 6700 to 9516 (wraps), 808 to 3624. Two polar caps (|lat| > 60) on a polar projection at 2048 px. Hero content pasted at the tile edges, minimum error seams, low frequencies locked to the guide, band heights corrected by zonal profile correlation.

## Shader plan (src/stops/jupiter/globe.ts, copy of src/bodies/planet.ts)

- Light = clamp(C(ndl) x L(mu)). Io shadow: ray from the surface point to the Sun against the moon sphere, umbra about 1.3 x with a soft penumbra, moon position uniform in world space.
- Band flow: jet(lat) from the zonal profile, lon shift = jet x rate x elapsed, elapsed clamped to +-12 s, about +-0.6 deg at the hold ends. GRS swirl about its own centre, elliptical and confined, also applied to the patch lookup.
- Wisp layer: its own tileable fine turbulence texture, drifts faster, alternates by band, zero mean modulation, soft shadow offset away from the Sun, faded above |lat| 60.
- Ground haze, patches and the diagnostic outputs stay as on Mars.

## Terrain view (own module `src/stops/jupiter/deck.ts`, not the engine Relief)

- Camera fit of `jupiter-close.jpg` from its horizon (`relief/camfit.py`, `camera-fit.json`): vfov 64, focal 1267.46 px at 2816 wide, nadir (-0.272, -0.885, -0.377), dip 4.44 deg, roll 17.09, camera height 1 unit, planet radius 331.95 units, horizon 25.8 units away. Plan: `horizonDeg 17.72, dipDeg 4.44, rollDeg 17.09`.
- Canvas: 5632 x 3168 in image pixels, the approved image at (1408, 792). Aprons by seedream_5, one edit per tile with the finished part pixel aligned and grey over the unfinished part (`relief/tiles.py`: left, right, bottom, bl, br; the top left triangle below the horizon is a mirror fill). A whole canvas outpaint at half size failed (the model redraws the picture at another size; nano_banana_pro pastes it as a rectangle). `relief/canvas.py`: seam step removal at the image edges, stars above the horizon replaced by a smooth rim table (polar, percentile along the horizon), image pixels kept to 0.4 deg above the fit horizon. `relief/build.py textures sky grid`: `deck-core.jpg` (approved, upscaled 2x), `deck-wide.jpg` (canvas), `sky-terrain.webp` (approved sky minus the deck texture in scene linear, mirrored below the horizon; sum with the deck is the approved picture), `deck-dist.bin` (705 x 397 float32: sphere distance x exp(-relief), relief = high pass of log depth from `tools/ml/depth.py` on the half size canvas, faded from 5 to 14 units), `src/stops/jupiter/relief.json`.
- Mesh: grid vertex = image ray x distance, so the judged frame is the approved picture by construction. Rays above the horizon sit at the tangent distance and carry the rim glow additively (alpha 0); the limb alpha is analytic from the current camera (ray miss distance against the sphere).
- Flow: closed form in ground coordinates (tangent frame at the storm centre): each vortex turns points by angle = rate x dt / (1 + q^4), q the elliptical radius; the texture is read at the turned point (difference of two projections, zero at the judged frame). GRS centre px (1132, 552), ground axes 1.12 x 2.18 units, four small vortices. Wisp layer: `wisp.png` in ground coordinates, turned faster, zero mean modulation plus a soft shadow toward the Sun. Lightning: eight seeded sites with closed form flash envelopes, quiet at the judged frame.
- Predictor `relief/predict.py` (reads the terrain params from `plan.ts`, `key=value` overrides): footprint of every pose in image pixels against the canvas (x -1408..4224, y -792..2376). Backing up needs the bottom apron fast (about 2000 px per unit), so the judged frame sits at u 0 with only 0.33 units behind it: `gather 0.6, forward 0.95` gives u -0.35..0.65 (slow after the dive, faster toward the rise). Forward needs no apron.
- Sky patch value = inverseFilmic(texel) x gain x skyGlow x skyExposure, so gain 2 with skyExposure 0.5 is unity.

## Terrain view: state at the milestone (06:00 UTC)

- Seam: `canvas.py seam()` takes a multiplicative tone ratio (approved pixels against tile) along the right, left and bottom image edges, applied outside with an exponential fall of 320 px and inside a 24 px blend band (ratios right 0.46 to 1.89, left 0.89 to 1.19, bottom 0.81 to 1.25). The right seam is gone.
- Sky: the rim glow table is the glow minus the sky background (median of 5 to 7.5 deg elevation per azimuth), so the sky patch keeps its background down to the horizon. The sky patch is built in `canvas.py` (image minus glow table from 0.4 deg up, mirrored about 0.4 deg below, `sky-lin.npy`); `build.py sky` only saves it. Deck above the horizon = (image minus blurred patch) x keep + glow x (1 minus keep), keep from 0.4 to 0.6 deg.
- `skyAim.terrain [0, -21.8, 31]` (band of the one sky where the approved picture has its band), `light.terrainSkyGlow 0.35` (the engine sky was 2.6 to 3.2 times the approved sky), `sky.json` terrain gain 5.714 (= 2 / 0.35, unity). The judged frame did not change.

## Dive and rise: patches (built 06:25 UTC)

- `assets-src/jupiter/patches/predict.ts MAPWIDTH [widthDeg:px ...]` with env `CENTRE=lon,lat`, `BRIEF=1`, `FEATHER`, `FADE`. It walks the dive and rise frames through the director and applies the shader's own rule per pixel (every 12th): texels = max of the two screen derivatives of the body direction x mapWidth / 2 pi; a patch level counts where its weight is at least 0.5 (feather 0.12, fade 1.02 to 1.5).
- Cover window (engine, 0.5 s): dive 3016 to 3031 (view switches at 3024), rise 3262 to 3277 (centre 3270). Frames that must hold G13: dive to 3015, rise from 3278.
- Camera altitude (planet radii): 3005 1.52, 3008 1.24, 3010 1.06, 3012 0.89, 3015 0.67, 3016 0.60, 3020 0.38, 3023 0.27; rise 3270 0.12, 3277 0.28, 3278 0.32, 3282 0.51, 3285 0.73, 3288 1.11, 3292 1.92, 3296 2.87.
- Wrap alone (8192): dive soft from 3007 to the cover (share up to 0.81 at 3015); rise soft from 3278 to 3289.
- The dive comes in obliquely: the point under the camera is 18 to 21 deg east of the storm until the cover; the rise leaves west. So the patches are centred between them, not on the storm.
- Final: `src/stops/jupiter/patches.json`, centre lon -12, lat -10; gnomonic levels wide 100 deg at 5120, mid 61.5795 deg at 4096, near 33.1816 deg at 4096 (half tangents twice apart). Predicted min texels per pixel outside the cover 1.21 (3015) and 1.26 (3278), soft share 0 on every frame. The globe shader has its own patch centre (`uPatchN/E/U`), apart from the swirl centre.
- Built with `assets-src/jupiter/patches/build.py` (recipe section "Dive patches"): wide = wrap resampled and ML upscaled, mid and near = one global repaint plus four tile repaints each, high pass locked onto the parent's low pass, then nested bottom up. Disk: full 50 MB, web 14 MB with the patches.
- A gnomonic patch is finer toward its edge (1 / cos squared radially), so one wide level serves the oblique far field.

## Dive and rise: cover and haze (in progress)

- Engine cover can only ramp over 8 frames per side. Own haze carries it further on both sides:
  - Orbit side, `globe.ts` veil (`look.veil`): a thin layer just above the cloud tops, opacity 1 - exp(-amount x pattern / mu), amount a closed form of camera altitude (from 1.5 down to 0.35 radii), so it gathers from frame 3006 and is gone again by 3290 in the rise. Pattern from `wisp.png` at 4 and 12 repeats in longitude, read with `textureLod`.
  - Terrain side, `deck.ts` fog (`look.deck.haze`): 1 - exp(-density x distance x pattern), pattern read part way along the ray so it streams, also over the first degrees above the horizon; density is `(1 - (t - diveCover) / clear)^power` and the mirror of it before the rise cover.
- `wisp.png` is loaded with repeat in u only (engine `ctx.texture`); `set.ts` sets `wrapT` to repeat on it. Before that the deck wisps and the veil were stretched into streaks wherever v left 0 to 1.
- The deck writes its color unpremultiplied (the pixels above the horizon add the approved picture's rim glow onto the sky); leave it so, the judged frame matches.

## Rise view and terrain speed (07:00 UTC)

- Rise `azimuthDeg 44` kept (`strip_rise3.jpg`): the storm upright, Io in front with its shadow drawn out toward the terminator, the lower half in night. Judged rise frame 3296 is this view.
- Terrain speed: the old values (`gather 0.6, forward 0.95, rise 0.37`) started the view at 0.0021 units per frame forward (hover) and ended at 0.0083. Mars uses `gather 0.22, rise 0.06`. The apron limits backing up to about 0.41 units (canvas x -1408, y 2376; a lower camera needs more bottom apron, so less climb allows more backing). ## The sky turns against the deck in the terrain view (07:45 UTC)

- The engine sets the terrain set's rotation to `frame x turn(time) x terrainFrame` (`film.ts` state()): the deck rides the spinning planet (`spinDegPerSecond 1.6`), the sky does not. So the sky and its patch move against the limb by about 1.07 deg per second in the pitch direction: at 3060 the patch horizon is 3.2 deg above the limb, at 3150 on it, after 3150 below it (stars set behind the limb). Camera height adds only 0.25 deg at 3060 (`out/work/jupiter/tools/dip.ts`, `lines.ts`: limb at x 1500 predicted y 452, seen 450; patch horizon seen at 375).
- So before the judged frame there is always a strip between the limb and the patch horizon that shows the sky patch below its horizon. Mirror fill: mirrored stars (the Builder's item 4). Star free mirror: a black belt under a detached line. Flat haze color: a flat belt with a hard top (`strip_band.jpg`). Now: haze color of the far deck per azimuth x (1 - exp(elev / 1.5 deg)), dark at the patch horizon, so it reads as glow over the limb (`strip_band2.jpg`).
- A star of the approved picture 0.3 deg above the horizon was baked into `deck-core.jpg` (dot with a dark red halo, seen riding on the limb at 3200 and 3239). `canvas.py` now takes stars out of the deck's sky rows (opening) and subtracts the star free blurred patch.
- `canvas.py` needs about 6 GB: it is killed (exit 137, no message through a pipe) when a render runs beside it. Run it alone and read `out/work/jupiter/canvas.log`.
- Do not `pkill -f batch` only: `strip.sh` and the npm child keep running. Kill `strip.sh`, `tools/still.ts`, `tools/compare.ts` too and check with `ps`.

No dark line, the glow starts softly under the patch horizon. This is the last take before READY; if it is not better it goes on the weak list.

### Items 2 and 3 (cover handover), in work (10:20 to 11:00 UTC)

Causes: (a) engine cover alpha has a threshold: 0.42 at 3028, 0.10 at 3029; (b) on the terrain side the cover had no shell, so it blacked the sky and the stars came back in one frame; (c) the limb is not the same curve on both sides of the cut: the orbit side sits at 0.045 radii (limb 73 deg from nadir), the terrain camera at 0.0045 to 0.0055 radii (84 deg), so at the cut the limb jumps up to 0.19 of the half height (about 100 px) at the frame sides while the centre matches (`out/work/jupiter/tools/hz.ts` prints the limb height at five columns per frame, `P='{"terrain.entryHeight":21}'` overrides plan values). Raising `entryHeight` and `exitHeight` to the true scale (21 and 25 units) was rejected: the deck mesh ends 5.2 deg over its own horizon at 25.8 units, so from 15 units it no longer reaches the horizon, the swoop changes with `entryHeight` (frame 2997 moved 10 to 20 px), and the dive side has no `entryDipDeg` to carry the extra dip.

What is built (all in the stop folder):
- `pall.ts`: `PALL_GLSL` is the engine cover's own pattern and alpha (copied formula, same focus, phase, flow, horizon and colours, computed by `Pall.apply` exactly as `src/director/film.ts` computes them), with its own amount `smooth(1 - |t - cut| / span)` (`look.deck.pall`: terrain side `clear` 0.6 s after the dive cut, `gather` 0.5 s before the rise cut, orbit side `orbitGather` 0.35 s and `orbitClear` 0.5 s). So the moving haze goes on for 18 frames past the cut and thins smoothly, where the engine cover ends after 5.
- Orbit side: the globe shader draws the pall over the globe inside `coverShell` (its own `rt`).
- Terrain side: `PallSea`, a full screen layer in the terrain group (render order 10, no depth, discards in raw diagnostic views). Its limb is an analytic sphere in camera space (`uPallSea`), and that sphere is morphed: at the cut it is the globe exactly as the orbit camera sees it at the cut (`Pall.cut`: orbit pose at the cover centre, minus 1e-6 s for the dive), and over `morph` 0.234 s (7 frames) it becomes the deck's own sphere (direction by slerp, altitude by a power law, shell from `coverShell` [1, 1.015] to `pall.shell` [1, 1.004]). While the morph weight is above 0 the deck is clipped by the same sphere (`held` in `deck.ts`), so it cannot stand above the limb at the frame sides. The weight is reset to 0 in every early return, because stills are rendered in any order.
- Plan: `coverSea` (deck sphere, `edge [0.955, 0.972]`): the engine cover on the terrain side stays 9 deg under the true horizon, inside the morphed limb at both cuts and in the frame corners; the band up to the limb is the pall's (same colour and pattern, alpha 1 at the cut). The sky above the limb is open on both sides; its brightness is the engine's `(1 - amount)^2` (0 at the cut, back over 8 frames on each side).
- Strips: `strip_cut0.jpg` (before), `strip_cut1_dive.jpg`, `strip_cut1_rise.jpg` (pall in the deck shader, limb not yet morphed), `strip_cut2.jpg` (morphed limb).

### Cover handover, second pass (10:45 UTC)

`strip_cut2.jpg`, `strip_cut2b.jpg`: limb continuous over both cuts (3023 to 3024 and 3269 to 3270), morph to the straight horizon done by 3030, haze pattern carried to about 3030, deck through by 3038. Two faults left and their fixes:
- The orbit side showed a thin bright yellow line on the limb (the additive haze shells outside the cover shell) that the terrain side did not have. Shell gain now times `(1 - pall amount)^2` (`clearing` in `set.ts`), so the rim comes back with the haze clearing (15 frames after the rise cut).
- Stars: the engine brings the sky back over 8 frames. `light.skyLow [0.045, 1.0, 0]` (a plan value the engine already reads, orbit view outside the hold): sky exposure follows the camera's altitude, 0 at the cut height (0.045 radii) and full at 1 radius, that is frame 3281 in the rise and from 3016 down in the dive. Frame 2997 is at 2.56 radii: unchanged.
- Spans: `pall.clear 0.8`, `pall.gather 0.6`, `deck.haze.clear/gather 0.7`.

- Item 4. At 3296 the night side is [3.5, 7.4, 10.9] and the sky right outside it is [26, 32, 42] (Milky Way): a darker disc cut out of a brighter sky over 8 px. No stair steps left on the lit limb at 2997 (crop T) with `edge.air [0.45, 0.0025, 0.4, 0.007]`. Added `look.nightRim`: on the night side (`dusk`, ndl from -0.05 to 0.2 at the limb point) the outer air falloff widens from 0.007 to 0.014 radii and a faint blue grey glow `[0.0035, 0.005, 0.0075] * (1 - mu)^3` lies on the limb, so stars dim through a falloff instead of stopping on an arc.
- Item 5. Io is now an analytic sphere: smooth feathered edge, no dark fringe (crop `f2997_io.png`). The streaks in its map at 3296 are the engine's stepped time samples (also on Jupiter's clouds there). Shadow: `out/work/jupiter/tools/shadow.ts` prints Io, the shadow axis and the sun height at the shadow centre per frame. Hold: ndl 0.51 at 2820, 0.39 at 2895, 0.23 at 2969; dive: 0.14 at 2997, axis off the planet from 3013; rise (old): axis off the planet (1.06 radii), so the "column" was the penumbra grazing the terminator. Fixes: (a) the stretch along the surface is capped: `off = length(vec2(true off axis distance, stretch * dot(p - centre, sun)))`, `stretch = 0.35 * (1 - smooth((ndl_centre - 0.12) / 0.24))`, zero at the judged hold frame (ndl 0.39), so the shadow closes into an ellipse of at most about 3.3 to 1; `uShadowAt` is computed in `set.ts` from the Sun direction and Io's position; (b) `shadowFade [0, 0.1]`; (c) Io's place in the rise is its own closed form: `io.riseDeg` at the rise's middle plus `moonDegPerSecond * (t - riseMid)` (the terrain view hides the change). First try 0.4 (2 deg at 3296): Io sat on the Great Red Spot and the shadow, seen from straight above, was a 3 to 1 column (ndl 0.33). Any place with a compact shadow (ndl over 0.5) puts Io or the shadow on the storm. Now `riseDeg 20.4` (22 deg at 3296): Io lit over the dusk zone at (1259, 358), away from the storm and off the disc centre; the shadow axis passes 1.05 radii from the centre, so no shadow falls on the planet in the rise.
- Item 6. The patch is minified (1.56 texels per pixel at 2997, 1.47 at the hold) and was read without mips: aliasing, stars as hard single pixels. Sky patches now load with mips (the engine reads them with `textureGrad`).
- Item 7. `terrain.side -0.5` (edge.ts: no border in frame at -0.55), storm `degPerSecond 9`, wisps 18 with a collar (`deck.collar [0.12, 0.45, 2.0]`: the turn reaches to 2 storm radii, hero pixels 0.12, wisps 0.45), wisp amount 0.2.
- Item 8. Final 3150 against approved, means over thirds: middle third -0.9 -1.5 -1.6, lower third -0.8 -1.1 -1.2 (darker by about one level; not less warm by the numbers). `deck.tone [1.03, 1.05, 1.055]` on the dark range (weight 1 below linear 0.08, 0 above 0.4).

## Round 17 result (my round 2 of 3), mail 19, 13:15 UTC. Round 3 is the last: what it holds is what ships

Judged on main at c08a38a. R1 7 (was 9). R3 7 (was 4; parts 9 and 7). R4 4 (was 6). R2 pass, R8 pass. G5, G13, G17 green. One severe, eight majors. Pass bar: R1, R3, R4 each at least 9, no severe or major open.

Corrections, worst first (critic's words in quotes, then the Builder's own):

1. HOLD SKY 2895 (R1 major). "stars and Milky Way dust lanes are noticeably blurred into soft 3-4px blobs instead of crisp 1-2px pinpoints, losing fine sky detail behind the sharp planet". Builder: confirmed at native size, x 1368 to 1824, y 216 to 649. Round 1 had this at 9. "Put the patch back to one texel per pixel at the hold framing (no mip level above 0 there, no resample through a smaller canvas)." Also 3150: "background pinpoint stars and faint Milky Way dust above the horizon are slightly softened" (R1 minor).
2. IO 2895 (R1 major; R3 minors; four jurors name the shadow). "an unnatural bright reddish-orange band appears on Io's shadowed right limb where -a has a dark cool-shaded terminator falling cleanly to black, and Io's surface texture is slightly softer". "the shadow is a soft oval stuck to the moon's night limb, with no crisp umbra" (2997 and 2895). Builder: x 1010 to 1250, y 560 to 760; the hero's shadow is a crisp, deep black ellipse; ours has an orange band on the night limb and a softer, greyer shadow. "Bring both back to the hero. The shadow's direction is the hero's and stays."
3. RISE 3274 to 3294 (R4 severe). "the globe pops to a different planet pose between these frames. At 3280 the bands run roughly horizontal ... By 3287 the bands run vertical, the lower half sits in deep night shadow, and Io has jumped". Builder: path is continuous, but from a limb close-up at 3274 to a small globe at 3287 in about 13 frames, ending with the bands upright. "Ease the pullback over many more frames so the turn can be followed, and take the roll out so the bands stay near level, as in the hold. The terminator and Io should come into view gradually, not between two samples."
4. DIVE 3010 to 3019 (R4 major). "the speed jumps. The globe grows smoothly through 2986 to 3010, then fills the frame in 9 frames". "Ease across 2994 to 3019." Minor: "the limb swings from the left edge to an arc across the top in about 3 frames" (3019 to 3022).
5. COVER EXIT 3035 to 3043 (R4 major). "color and exposure break. The cover ends in a flat warm tan haze, and the terrain opens dark, high-contrast and blue-shadowed with a bright warm horizon glow, so the change in tone reads as a cut". Builder: the cover is ten frames of soft tan with only a limb edge (3021 to 3030). "Carry the haze into the first terrain frames and thin it over 10 to 15 frames; keep cloud structure readable inside the cover."
6. TERRAIN 3060 to 3239 (R4 major). Brandon: clear forward movement and depth across the whole length. "the terrain reads as a flat push-in on a single image. The vortex, the near cloud tops and the horizon arc all scale and slide together with no visible parallax between foreground and horizon". Ladder: more layers with what they hide painted in, independent motion (haze between layers, moving shadows, flow), and if the relief cannot give it, rung 4, the foreground third in true 3D. Minor: "the vortex barely rotates. Its spiral arms keep the same arrangement for about 180 frames".
7. BEFORE THE RISE COVER 3240 to 3253 (R4 major). "exposure climbs fast. The terrain brightens and lifts toward pale tan over about 13 frames before the blur starts, which looks like a fade-to-white crossfade". "Tie haze and exposure to altitude over a longer stretch."
8. RIBBONS at 3296 and NEAR CLOUD BANKS at 3150 (R3 major, two). "Several broad cloud lanes stretch into long, smooth ribbons with much less cross-lane detail than the compact red vortex. The bands look dragged across the globe rather than layered." "The curled ridges turn into soft, brushlike streaks with little small-scale structure, even beside the brighter cloud tips. The nearest clouds look less resolved than the middle-distance storm rim."

Minors, after 1 to 8: sunlit limb glow "slightly wider and hotter"; night limb "soft 2-3px feathered fringe and faint night-side cloud bleed"; Io in the rise "stops at a nearly straight, abrupt boundary above a featureless dark half" (3296); "The brightest strip merges into a creamy band that wipes out the tops of adjacent cloud layers" (3150, upper left horizon); "Some bright points have swollen or slightly elongated profiles" (3296 star field); hold "the globe shows no visible rotation" (a slow turn inside what the hero covers).

Travel: in the travel that arrives (2798 to 2816) the patch's Milky Way band appears out of a plain star field. Venus set `skyAim` in its plan so the shared sky's band lies under the patch's band (`src/stops/venus/plan.ts`, `skyAim.orbit`). Do the same in `src/stops/jupiter/plan.ts` if it can be done without moving the hold or the terrain frame.

Rules unchanged: sky patches fade into the shared star sky outside the approved border and change nothing inside; the approved image wins in the hold and terrain frame when a critic faults what the approved image itself shows; stepped copies in fast frames are an engine matter; every number from config/showcase.json; every generated asset has a recipe; only my folders on agent/jupiter. Engine batch 13 (sky across the travels) is coming; merge origin/main before READY.

READY format: `READY <commit> round 3`, then changes per item number above, what is still weak, measures (G5, G13, G17, mirrorcheck). Before sending: render the dive, rise and both cover sheets and the terrain sheet at review tier and look at every frame; put hold 2895 beside the hero at native size in the sky and at Io.

Binaries: one commit of `public/assets/jupiter/` in round 3 (sky patches will change for item 1).

### Item 3, rise: stop-local path (13:20 to 13:37)

- Engine parameters alone cannot slow the visible pullback: `rise()` blends to `after()` over u 0.6 to 1, which fixes the pullback at about 15 frames and leaves 3298 to 3314 idle; with `level: true` the bands end 80 deg off level.
- Decision: `class JupiterPlan extends StopPlan` in `src/stops/jupiter/plan.ts` overrides `rise(time)` only. No engine file touched; the film calls `p.rise(t)`. Constants in `climbOut`.
  - Terrain side (before the cover centre 3270): the engine pose times a camera roll about the view axis, `bankDeg * ramp(c - bankLead, c, t)^bankPower`.
  - Orbit side: position on the engine's own curve (`from`, `m = (ratio^e - 1)/(ratio - 1)`, `across * m^hug`) straight to `after(time)` with `e = 1 - (1 - x)^climb`, x over 3270 to 3314. Orientation: slerp from the horizon-carried pose at the current position (roll `terrain.rollDeg + bankDeg`) to `lookAt(position, end target, end up)` with weight `1 - (1 - x)^turn`. Lens `smooth(x)` from 64 to 30. End equals `after(time)` with zero relative rate (gap at 3314.999: 2.7e-6 units, 0 deg).
  - `rise.level` removed, `settle 2`, `tilt 0.5` (used only by `risePose`, the travel's start: bands level there).
- Numbers (tool `out/work/jupiter/tools/rise2.ts`, env `K` for the constants): globe size in half heights 5.25 at 3270, 3.42 at 3276, 2.60 at 3280, 1.70 at 3287, 1.07 at 3296, 0.68 at 3314; change of log size per frame 0.07 falling evenly to 0.004 (before: 3.12 at 3274, 0.98 at 3288, idle from 3298). Bands on screen: 78 deg at 3270, 33 at 3276, 15 at 3280, -2 at 3287, -8 at the end (before: 80 deg off at the end).
- First take `bankDeg -50, lead 0.8, turn 4`: strip `strip_rise_r3a.jpg` reads as one continuous pullback, bands level by 3287, Io and the terminator come in from the right edge over 3291 to 3296. Fault found: **the Sun enters the top of the frame at 3269** (terrain side). The engine's Sun is not dimmed by the cover; without bank it sits 32 px above the top edge at 3269, at radius 598 px from the centre, 17 deg right of straight up, so a roll between -8 and +42 deg keeps it out (tool `out/work/jupiter/tools/sun.ts`).
- Second take, in the tree: `bankDeg -32, bankLead 1.0, turn 5`: Sun 40 px above the top edge at 3269 (108 at 3262), turn rate 9.1 deg per frame at 3271 under 0.93 cover, 5.7 at 3276, 3.8 at 3280, 1.6 at 3287.

### Item 1, hold sky (13:16 to 13:30)

- Cause: the patch at the hero's scale is 1.47 texels per pixel at the hold; with mips the engine's `textureGrad` taps land between level 0 and 1. Round 1 had no mips (crisp hold, aliased 2997).
- `sky/scale.py MASTER NAME OUT [gain] [knee]`: one Lanczos step from the 3520 x 1980 master to 2400 x 1350 (one texel per pixel at 1920), then a radial frequency gain in linear light, `1 + gain * ((f - knee)/(0.5 - knee))^1.5`.
- Measured on final 2895, sky box x 1500 to 1900, y 100 to 500, film over hero (Lanczos to 1920): with the plain 2400 patch the transfer is 1.0 to 1.09 up to 0.2 cycles per pixel (post sharpen), 0.91 at 0.25, 0.75 at 0.325, 0.60 at 0.4, 0.45 at 0.475 (bilinear tap, the four taps of the engine, the pixel box of 16 samples). High pass std in the Builder's box: hero 12.8, plain 2400 patch 10.6, READY head 10.1. The inverse is gain 1.3, knee 0.2. Takes in `out/work/jupiter/sky-take/full/` (hold 4.2 MB, terrain 1.3 MB), not yet rendered.

### Item 6, terrain depth: findings before any change (13:30)

- The deck's relief is tiny: heights of the displaced mesh are -0.05 to +0.05 units (std 0.015) with the camera 1 unit up. One plane moves as a homography, which is exactly "a flat picture". Rung 1 was never really climbed.
- Plan: (a) `look.deck.depth`: scale the relief at load (`dist = hit * (dist/hit)^k`, judged frame unchanged by construction), try k 3 to 5 and look for stretch at the tower silhouettes; (b) a wider sideways arc with counter yaw in an own `terrain()` so the foreground, the storm and the horizon slide against each other; (c) faster storm rotation; (d) haze between layers only if (a) to (c) leave time.

### 14:22 to 14:45 UTC: exit texels (3240 to 3261) on the arc path

- With the arc the terrain itself passes (3239 0.48 percent) but the exit does not: soft 3.9 percent at 3251, 9.5 to 10.5 at 3257, 6.4 to 11.5 at 3261 (G13 allows 2). The soft zone is the Red Spot itself (`tex3257_pair.png`): by then the camera has come close to the storm and looks down on it; the approved pixels of the storm (core, 2.93 texels per pixel at the judged frame) run out.
- The measure is `sqrt(max(|dUV/dx|^2, |dUV/dy|^2))` in texels (`src/diagnostics/diag.ts`), on the warped coordinates of the deck. A smooth sphere predictor (`out/work/jupiter/tools/pred.ts`) gives a minimum of 1.28 to 1.41 on these frames and no soft pixel, so the soft share comes from the relief facets and the vortex warp on top of a thin base margin. The predictor does not reproduce the measured share over time: use renders.
- Single factors at 3257 (baseline 9.5 to 10.5 percent): exit pitch 5 instead of 9: 9.4; terrain pitch -3: 9.3; climb bank 0: 10.6; yaw 3: 6.8; old straight side path: 10.3; no lean: 8.0; depth 1: 8.2; exitHeight 1.5: 5.8; vortex 9 deg per s with collar 0.12: 6.3; vortex 14 with collar 0.12: 8.9; vortex 9 with collar 0.3: 8.5. No single one is enough; the round 2 head passed only because every one of them was at its mild value.
- The main driver is how far forward the camera is at the exit: `gather 0, exitForward 0` (the glide at constant speed, no extra push before the cover; forward position at 3257 0.59 instead of 0.88): 3239 0.14, 3251 2.68, 3257 1.21, 3261 1.37. With `exitHeight 2` as well: 3251 1.76, 3257 0.81, 3261 2.42. Variants with a slightly decelerating glide (gather -0.15), yaw -5 and collar 0.2 running (`/tmp/exp7.log`).

## Round 25 result (my round 3 of 3), mail 24, 19:52 UTC. Jupiter is marked finished on this build. Phase 6 polish list follows

- Judged on main at 530168f (my 3f167e0 merged). R1 10 (was 7). R3 7 (parts 8 and 7). R4 5 (was 4). R2 pass (3 of 10 against 2 of 10), R8 pass. G5, G13, G17 green. No severe, five majors. The pass bar does not hold, the three rounds are used, the kept build is round 25. Its review clip renders from main 1fd7e80. Nothing of mine goes onto main again before Phase 6. Full text: `scorecards/round-025/SUMMARY.md`, open items 33 to 37 in `kit/PROGRESS.md`.
- TASK NOW (mail 24): the Phase 6 polish list for Jupiter, on agent/jupiter only (looks on agent/jupiter-looks), worst first. Phase 6 has at most three polish rounds for the whole film, "so each item must arrive measured and looked at by you at review tier, with the judged hold 2895 and terrain 3150 unchanged unless the item is about them."
  1. RISE COVER 3265 to 3276 (two R4 majors). "the rise cover ends on a flat, band-free beige disk. The bands, small detail and star field then pop in within two frames, and the color jumps from grey-beige to bright cream by 3280". And: "the limb goes from a shallow slope rising to the right to a steep arc in one frame [3270-3271]. That reads as a snap in camera roll or direction, not one continuous move". Cure wanted: the roll starts in the last terrain frames and is eased over the whole climb so that no frame turns more than about 5 degrees, the banded map shows at low contrast through the cover and comes up over 8 to 12 frames, colour and exposure go from the cover's tone to the globe's over the same frames. The engine will have a cover window per stop (`coverWindow`, engine batch 16, Phase 5); say how many frames I want.
  2. SKY IN BOTH COVERS 3021 to 3026 and 3267 to 3274 (R4 major). "the stars and Milky Way drop to pure black during the dive cover, then come back". Engine: sky exposure times (1 - cover amount) squared; changed for all stops in Phase 5. Do nothing in my folder, but tell the Builder what my covers need (a floor value, or the sky drawn above the limb only).
  3. DIVE 3002 to 3010 and the end of the rise 3274 to 3287 (R4 major). "Jupiter grows from about half the frame to filling it in 8 frames, after a nearly static hold. The dive start feels like a lurch, not an eased acceleration. The matching end of the rise ... is just as abrupt". Cure wanted: the acceleration begins inside the last second of the hold (2970 on, without moving 2895) and ramps over 30 frames or more; the fastest part fully under the cover; the settle of the rise spread to about 3300. Frames 2997 and 3296 are judged frames: keep them sharp.
  4. TERRAIN 3150, near folds below and right of the storm, crops D and E (R3 major). "Several neighboring cloud folds curl into similarly smooth, hollow tubes. The illuminated tips retain fine detail, but the broad surfaces between them look smeared and sculpted rather than turbulent." Cure wanted: finer wisps, pockets of density and a second thin cloud layer generated into the near folds, the approved pixels kept as the low frequencies, G17 at 3150 not lower than now (median 0.973).
  5. Minors, after 1 to 4: the south limb's falloff at 2895 and 2997 ("drops from cloud brightness to space in about six pixels"), Io softer and "slightly glossy" beside the deck and "slightly like a cutout" at 3296, far bands "flatten into long, nearly parallel streaks" at 3150, the blue glow "reads as an overlay" at 3150 crop U, "fuzzy, elongated flecks" among the stars at the lower left of 3296, stepped copies in the blur at 3019 and 3265 to 3267, "little parallax" between near cloud tops and horizon in the terrain view.

### MAIL 45 (00:17 UTC, 2026-11-22, from /root), answer to POLISH MINORS 1964691, verbatim core

"Received: south limb and Io night side done, 3150 unchanged, measures green. Thank you.

Your question: yes. Build the true foreground layer for parallax in the terrain view. Brandon's order for every stop puts motion first, and the terrain view "has to show clear forward movement and depth across its whole length". The ladder in kit/04-LOOK.md section 9, "A terrain view reads as a flat picture being zoomed", is the path: rung 2 (split more layers and paint in what they hide), rung 3 (independent motion: drifting haze in front of and between layers, moving shadows, flow), rung 4 (the foreground third of the view rebuilt in true 3D, the relief kept for the middle and far distance). Start at the rung you are on and go down as far as the pictures need.

Rules for it:
1. Inside your three folders only. If it needs an engine hook, write it as an engine request in assets-src/jupiter/ENGINE-REQUESTS.md and tell me; do not touch shared files.
2. Frame 3150 stays the fitted pose of the approved close image. Check it against the kept still: the match with the approved image must not get worse (your own mean difference and G17 at 3150 both reported before and after).
3. What the new layer uncovers behind it is painted in. No stretched edge, no doubled edge, no hole at any frame of 3060 to 3239. Look at every eighth frame of the terrain view at the draft tier, then at the review tier at the two ends and the middle.
4. The foreground must move against the middle distance by an amount a viewer sees at once across the whole length of the view, not only at one end. Give me the numbers: screen motion in pixels per frame of a near point and of a far point at 3070, 3150 and 3230.
5. The recipe of every new generated or painted asset is committed beside it (kit/06-ASSETS.md).
6. Nothing goes onto main before Phase 6. It is judged in the Phase 6 polish rounds by pictures beside the kept ones.

The dive pall gathering 4 frames earlier (orbitGather 0.35 to 0.5): go on with the drafts and send the report with pictures as planned.

Report with a message that starts POLISH READY and names the commit on agent/jupiter, with a contact sheet of the terrain view before and after."

In the climb 3240 to 3262 (still outside the cover) it grows to hundreds of px, so the painted plate under a lifted layer must be wide (about 300 canvas px below each edge) or cover the whole lifted region.
- DESIGN: terraces, not flaps. A flap hinged close to its crest stretches by half its length; a terrace (everything on the near side of a contour moved nearer by one factor, the step tapering out sideways over 500 px or more) has no stretch at all and makes the whole near field faster, not only an edge. Layers, far to near: the deck as it is (drawing the painted plate under the layers above), tier 1 = the near bank inside the approved image below the contour C1 (left apron cumulus top, top edge of the cumulus cluster at canvas 1490..1770 x 1850..1990, along one streak to the wave crest 2060..2480 x 1955..2000, tapering out to the right by about x 2800 where the picture has no crest to cut along), tier 2 = the bottom apron's cumulus below C2 (canvas y 2480 to 2700). Lifts to try: tier 1 s 0.75 to 0.7, tier 2 s 0.55 to 0.5.
- Shading of a lifted layer is computed at the deck position of the same texel (reach, haze, limb), only the drawn position is nearer, so 3150 is the same picture by construction wherever the matte is 1.
- New assets planned: `deck-tiers` (the mattes, built by script from the contours) and `deck-under` (the plate: the deck painted on under the lifted layers, grey window fill as for the aprons). Budget: full set 756 of 1400 MB, web 199 of 450; the plate at 2x over about 3000 x 1300 canvas px adds about 85 MB full, 21 MB web.

### 01:40 to 02:00 UTC: CAUSE FOUND: the deck texture slides over the mesh (the collar turn); the lift now follows the texture

- The mismatch was not the camera, not the depth test and not the relief. The fragment shader of the deck moves the picture over the mesh: `px = vPx + (toPx(swirl(vGround)) - toPx(vGround)) * nearby`, with `uTurn = degPerSecond * calm(time - terrain mid)` (the storm's collar of `look.deck.collar` plus the four small eddies). In the foreground the picture slides by about 420 canvas px between 3060 and 3150 and by 400 more to 3239 (about 4.6 canvas px per frame). My model left this out. With the slide added (`lib.py`: `ground`, `swirl`, `topx`, `warp(frame)`; frame time is `(frame + 0.25) / fps`, the shutter middle) the model lands on the render: SIFT matches kept 3150 final against the kept review frames 3060, 3093, 3125, 3190 (`truth3.py`): median error 0.9 to 1.8 px of 1920 (it was 34 to 70 px).
- So in batches a and b the mattes (looked up at the slid picture place) and the lift (a vertex attribute fixed in the mesh) were hundreds of px apart away from 3150: that was the flat cut at 3060 and every other fault of those batches. All numbers of an4, an5, an6 and the old `motion.ts` are void.
- FIX (working tree): the tier vertex shader computes the same slide at the vertex and takes the lift from the slid picture place (`uTierRise`, `uTierLift`, `uTierTaper`; shared GLSL `WARP_GLSL` for `toPx` and `swirl`, the fragment code is unchanged). The `aLift` attribute and `deck-tiers.bin` are gone; the two tier meshes draw every cell below canvas row `top` 1280 (the matte discards the rest; the model finds matte cells up to row 1488 at 3024). `tiers.json` gained `top`, lost `gap` and `under`.
- Batch c (lifts 0.28 and 0.2; `out/work/view/fgd/f0N-c.png`, `sbs_c_3060.jpg`, `sbs_c_3190.jpg`, `cropc3060.jpg`, `cropc3093.jpg`, layers `lay_c.jpg`): the cumulus bank stands up as one mass, clean edges at 3060, 3093, 3125; no flat cut. The layer is in frame through the whole view: bottom left corner at 3060, bottom left to middle at 3125, the bottom band at 3190, a wedge at the lower right at 3223 to 3239.
- Fault seen in batch c at 3223: a dotted bright line on the uncovered deck where the bank stood (the plate began 1 px inside the contour, so 3 px of the bright rim stayed on the base). CURE: the plate weight on the base has the same edge as the matte (`under = matte * band * left limit`), so the base shows the plate wherever the tier covers it; at 3150 the result differs from the approved picture only inside the 2 px feather by at most a quarter of (plate - picture). Also a small doubled patch at the end of the sideways taper (plate ended at taper 0.1, matte at 0.02): same cure. Layers view after the cure at 3190: no red at all.
- Layers view of deck.ts now marks: plate on the base violet, plate on tier 1 teal, and RED where a layer shows picture that a nearer tier carries and no plate replaces (a doubled picture). `count.py TAG frames` counts red px (and after a 3 x 3 erosion), the share of the frame per layer, and the G13 count from a texels view.
- Verified model numbers, px per frame of 1920 (`track.py`, and the committed `relief/motion.ts`, which now has the slide and agrees): the foreground already moved before the layer because of the slide (crest on the base 3.4 at 3070, 5.7 at 3150, 5.2 at 3230; far band 0.78, 0.75, 0.5). With lifts 0.28 and 0.2 the crest on tier 1 moves 3.81 (3070), 6.71 (3150), 8.05 (3230, below the frame from about 3215), the lobe 5.28, 6.50, 5.04. Against the deck right behind it the tier gains 0.4 px per frame at 3070, 1.1 at 3150, 2.0 at 3190.
- Uncover model (`an7.py`, tier always in front, which holds because the tiers are the deck scaled toward the fitted eye): deepest uncovered deck inside C1 158 px to 3228 (band full to 170), 185 to 187 at 3234 to 3246 (a few cells at the frame bottom); tier 1 under tier 2 at most 67. Left of canvas x 1444 (no plate there) nothing is uncovered in frame.
- Late frames, to judge: at 3223 the bank is magnified 1.2 to 1.5 times (px of 1920 per canvas px) and its striated lobe stands as tall pale streaks at the frame bottom (they are in the kept frame too, smaller). G13 to check with the texels view at 3223 to 3254.

### 02:00 to 02:12 UTC: batches d, e, f: the sideways taper sheared the roll, a hole at the mesh bottom in the dive

- Time base of my tools: the engine renders frame f at `(f + 0.25) / fps` (shutter fraction 0.5, `src/core/engine.ts`), so `fp.ts`, `lib.py` and `motion.ts` use +0.25 now. `truth3.py`: median error of the model against SIFT matches 1.5 / 1.8 / 1.3 / 0.9 px at 3125 / 3093 / 3060 / 3190.
- Batch d (plate edge = matte edge, lifts 0.28 and 0.2, bank taper [2450,2950]): the dotted bright line at 3223 is gone; layers views: no red at 3190, 3223, 3239, 3254; 125 to 150 px of 1 px lines at 3060 and 3093 (0 after a 3 x 3 erosion, nothing to see in the beauty). Texels views (my count by the G13 rule): 3190 0, 3223 0, 3239 0.05 percent, 3254 0.78 percent soft (3254 was 0.78 before the layer too, the soft patch is at x 260 to 595, y 976 to 1079, not on a tier).
- FAULT in batch d at 3223 and 3239 (`n3239d.jpg`, pushed): the breaking roll at canvas x 2550 to 2700 stood as a thin blade. Cause: the sideways taper of the bank's lift ran across it (0.25 at its left end, 0.14 at its right), so its left end dropped further than its right end and the diagonal roll turned upright. Batch e, taper [2760,3300] (roll at full lift): roll whole, but the dark swirl right of it and its lightning glows were drawn out downward (shear 130 px over about 450). Batch f, taper [2760,4060] (the whole width of the plate box): the same shift spread over 1300 px; roll whole, glows keep their shape, no line along the straight part of the contour (`n3223f.jpg`, `n3239f.jpg`, `sbs_f_3223.jpg`). Tier 1 now spans the frame bottom from the roll to the right edge in the late frames (6.8 to 7.5 percent of the frame at 3223 to 3239, 14.7 at 3190). The note of batch a, "a hard straight step in the dark night side", was the mesh fixed lift, not the straight contour.
- Band of the bank's plate widened to [200,250] (deepest uncovered deck by the model 185 to 187 px at 3234 to 3246). The plate under the bank is painted to canvas row 2292 (windows a1, a2), the band never goes deeper than that where the lift is above 0.01.
- FAULT in the dive, batch e layers views: a red strip (doubled picture) at the frame's bottom left corner, 13 px high at 3036, 24 at 3044, 21 at 3052 (up to 225 px wide), none from 3060. Cause: the lift went to zero at picture row 3168, but early in the view the picture is slid up over the mesh, so the mesh's own bottom edge carried lift up to 0.35 and hung in front of the base. CURE: the vertical ramp takes the lower of the picture row and the mesh row (`max(at.y, aPx.y)` in the vertex shader), so the tiers always meet the base at the mesh bottom. Cost by the model (`an12.py`): ramp factor of tier 2 in frame at 3060 median 0.33 instead of 0.45, at 3093 0.51 instead of 0.55; tier 1 0.80 instead of 1.0 at 3060, 0.97 at 3093; no change from about 3109.
- `relief/motion.ts` follows (taper, mesh row). Roll (canvas 2600,2010) on its tier: 5.38 / 6.84 / 5.62 px per frame at 3070 / 3150 / 3230 (on the base it was 4.97 / 5.64 / 3.94); crest (2300,1990): 3.81 / 6.71 / 8.05 (below the frame from about 3215); mid deck 2.66 / 4.39 / 3.76; storm rim 2.02 / 1.83 / 1.29; far band 0.78 / 0.75 / 0.52.

### MAIL 63 (03:10 UTC, no reply needed): engine batch 18 on main at a67c9c34, merged as 172522f (tree holds main 0622510)

- "Merge it into your branch ..., build, tests. It changes nothing in your hold, dive and terrain frames ...; your judged rise frame gets a slightly higher sky lift (0.74) and was looked at beside batch 17."
- "One finding to check in your own shaders, from the Saturn agent. Frames that do not repeat from one fresh page to the next (one level on a few edge pixels) came from this: the harness GPU shades 2 x 2 pixel quads; a pixel that hit `discard` stops storing to variables, so a local first assigned after the discard holds stale memory, and `dFdx`, `dFdy`, `fwidth` and `texture()` without an explicit level read it for the live neighbours of the quad. Rule: no `discard` before the last statement of a fragment shader (carry a flag and discard at the end), and no derivative or implicit level `texture()` on a value first assigned inside a per pixel branch. If you find the pattern in your folder, fix it with your next commit and say in your next READY which frames changed and by how many pixels. G3 asks for pixel identity across fresh page loads."
- 03:10: chain B stopped (it had made the review sheet of the terrain view and the review still 3150 on main 3d48581; those pictures do not count any more, the looks pushed at 03:07 name 3d48581 honestly). `git merge origin/main` at 03:12: merge commit 172522f, origin/main 0622510 (holds a67c9c34).
- The pattern IS in my folder: `deck.ts` line 180 `if (matte <= 0.0) discard;` in the new tier code (beauty path, before the derivatives of the wisps and the detail), not committed yet; and behind `uDiag >= 2` (diagnostic views only, no beauty pixel) in `deck.ts` 250, `io.ts` 80, `pall.ts` 91, `globe.ts` 225. To fix: carry a flag, discard as the last statement.

- E3 (plan.ts only): `skyGain(time)` on the orbit side of the rise returns `max(now, (1 - base cover amount)^veil.sky) / now`, where `now` is the `skyLow` share at the camera's height and the base cover amount is the engine's own window law (symmetric about the cut); `skyUnderCover` returns 1 from the rise cut on (the law is already in the gain). So the sky leaves and returns by the same law about the cut: .376 at 3265, .297, .211, .124, .047 at 3269, 0 at 3270, .047 at 3271, .124, .211, .297, .376 at 3275, .44, .48, .5 at 3278 (before: .0003 at 3271, .007, .020, .042, .077, .123, .181, .249 at 3278, .40 at 3280). Black only on the cut frame, so the swap of the two skies (172 degrees apart) still happens in the dark.

- Now (power 4): radius 489 (2970), 491 (2980), 508 (2990), 549 (2997, 12 percent over the hold), motion 0.6 (2980), 3.4 (2990), 7.64 (2997), 10.98 (3000), 18.7 (3004), altitude 0.599 (3014), 0.331 (3017).
- Law tried: on-screen ln radius `s0 + (s1 - s0)(m0 u + A G(u) + B u^p)`, `G` the integral of a smooth step that reaches 1 at `u1` (`G = u1 (x^3 - x^4 / 2)` with `x = u / u1` below 1, `u1 (x - 1/2)` above), `B = 1 - m0 - A (1 - u1 / 2)`. An early push that eases in over `u1` of the dive and then holds its rate, with the plunge power on top; the rate never falls.
- Candidates with 2997 at or under about 9 px: `A 0.35, u1 0.22, p 6`: radius 638 at 2997 (30 percent over the hold), motion 8.67, but the rate sits flat at about 5.2 px from 2980 to 2988 (reads as two moves). `A 0.46, u1 0.4, p 8`: radius 630 at 2997 (29 percent), motion 8.83, motion rises almost evenly 0.1 (2970), 3.45 (2980), 7.06 (2990), 8.83 (2997), 10.55 (3000), 18.9 (3006), 36.7 (3010), 88 (3014), 153 (3017), 189 (3019), 213 (3023); altitude 0.691 (3014), 0.427 (3017): higher than now before the cover, so the texel risk before the cover is smaller; faster than now only under the cover. Plain powers in place of the smooth step (`A u^q`, q 1.4 to 2.5) give less radius for the same motion or start with a kick.

### 11:12 to 11:20 UTC: dive trial law changed before it is built (offline, nothing under src touched): constant acceleration in place of the eased push

- Read the three paces side by side (now | eased push `A 0.46, u1 0.4, p 8` | quadratic lead `A 0.49, q 2, p 8`; `divepace.ts` takes `{"A","q","p"}`). The eased push holds its growth rate nearly flat from 2989 to 2997 (d ln r 0.0150 → 0.0167, +11 percent in 8 frames, after +100 percent in the 9 frames before and +43 percent in the 8 after): push, cruise, plunge. That is the two move reading I rejected in the first candidate, only milder.
- The quadratic lead (`want = s0 + (s1 - s0)(m0 u + lead u^2 + (1 - m0 - lead) u^power)`) gives a growth rate that rises in a straight line from the first frame of the dive and then bends up into the plunge: d ln r 0.0034 (2975), 0.0063 (2980), 0.0092 (2985), 0.0123 (2990), 0.0157 (2995), 0.0204 (3000), 0.0279 (3005), 0.0416 (3010), 0.0606 (3014), 0.0824 (3017), 0.1019 (3019). No flat part, no dip in the acceleration. This is "an eased acceleration" in the plain sense of mail 24.
- Price against the eased push: the disc at 2997 is 610 px in place of 630 (25 percent over the hold in place of 29; now 549, 12 percent). Motion at 2997 8.74 px (now 7.64, eased push 8.83; soft from 10).
- Chosen for the trial: `plunge = { power: 8, lead: 0.48 }` (lead 0 and power 4 give the READY head's path). Numbers offline (`out/work/jupiter/trial/after-pace-offline.txt`): radius 493 (2975), 505 (2980), 524 (2985), 552 (2990), 591 (2995), 610 (2997), 644 (3000), 723 (3005), 852 (3010), 1032 (3014), 1263 (3017), 1503 (3019), 2435 (3023); motion 1.62 (2975), 2.97 (2980), 5.50 (2990), 8.74 (2997), 9.41 (2998), 10.18 (2999), 11.12 (3000), 19.1 (3005), 42.6 (3010; now 54.0), 97 (3014; now 124), 156 (3017), 180 (3019), 198 (3023; now 158); altitude 0.651 (3014; now 0.599), 0.393 (3017; now 0.331), 0.249 (3019; now 0.204), 0.067 (3023; now 0.062). Under the cover the camera is nearer to today's heights than with the eased push (0.271 at 3019), so the cover frames change less.
- Other candidates at the same blur: `A 0.50, q 2, p 10` 614 px at 8.75 (a sharper plunge, more speed under the cover); `A 0.44, q 1.8, p 8` 617 px at 8.48 (rate rises as u^0.8, a harder start). Not taken: the gain is 1 percent of radius.

- minor, pair-01-b.png (jupiter.hold 2895), Right-hand terminator and dark disk (pair-02-b crops C, E, G and I): "A few isolated curls and thin blue streaks remain legible too far into the night side; the reference rolls into a more continuous dark mass." - minor, pair-01-b.png (jupiter.hold 2895), Sky, particularly the sparse left field and bright galactic band to the right (pair-02-b crops B through H): "Many stars look brighter and rounder, while the fine dusty texture in the galactic band reads a little softer." Fix named: "Reduce star bloom and recover the distinction between crisp pinpoints and diffuse galactic dust."
- minor, pair-03-b.png (jupiter.terrain 3150), Sky above the diagonal horizon (pair-04-b crops C and I): "The brightest stars are more swollen and conspicuous than the reference's finer points, making the upper-right sky look busier." Fix named: "Scale back point-source bloom and brightness while keeping the faint galactic structure in place."
- minor, pair-03-b.png (jupiter.terrain 3150), Storm rim, sunlit shoulders and lower-left foreground curls (pair-04-b crops A, B, D, F, G and H): "The vortex and cloud banks line up, but some of the thinnest lit edges and dark grooves blend together more than in the reference, reducing the sense of relief." Fix named: "Restore selective microcontrast along the amber ridges and foreground folds without sharpening the shadow floor."
- minor, pair-03-b.png (jupiter.terrain 3150), Lower-right blue-lit cloud pockets (pair-04-b crop E): "The blue glow is slightly more diffuse around its brightest cores, with less separation between the narrow illuminated filaments and surrounding black folds." Fix named: "Tighten the blue bloom around the cores and recover the fine filament edges."
R3-jupiter-part1, reviewer_1153, score 6:
- major, image-05.png (jupiter.dive 2997), Io's shadow at the terminator, right of centre (crops E and I in image-06, P in image-07): "the shadow is a hard-edged black oval that runs past the terminator into the night side and shows up as a darker blob on an already dark limb. It reads as a sticker pasted over the shading, not a shadow cast onto cloud" Fix named: "project the shadow onto the sphere so it stretches and fades with the terminator, give it a soft penumbra, and hide it where it falls on the night side"
- major, image-01.png (jupiter.hold 2895), southern limb and south polar region (crops S and T in image-04): "the south cap turns into a smeared, desaturated brown-grey surface with crater-like ovals. It looks like a rocky moon texture rather than Jovian cloud bands, and the detail is much softer than in the equatorial bands" Fix named: "carry the banded cloud texture with festoons and small ovals to the pole at matching resolution, and avoid crater-like rimmed spots"
- major, image-05.png (jupiter.dive 2997), southern limb (crops T and U in image-08): "the same soft, low-detail pole with crater-like spots, plus a bright rim along the lower limb where the night side meets space. The lighting band ends in a fairly hard line" Fix named: "use the same polar texture fix, and make the limb glow fall off smoothly into the night side"
- minor, image-05.png (jupiter.dive 2997), Milky Way at the far right edge (crop W in image-08, crop F in image-06): "two round, opaque black blobs in the bright star cloud look like holes or stamped artifacts, not dust lanes" Fix named: "remove the blobs or turn them into irregular, wispy dust lanes"
- minor, image-01.png (jupiter.hold 2895), Io in both frames (crop A in image-02, crop P in image-07): "Io has a smooth, plastic, airbrushed look with blotchy spots and little surface detail at native size" Fix named: "add finer surface detail, sharper volcanic features and a slight limb darkening"
- minor, image-01.png (jupiter.hold 2895), cloud bands across the disk (crops A, I, J, N): "the turbulent swirls are painterly and slightly airbrushed, with some soft, smeared zones between the sharp eddies" Fix named: "raise high-frequency cloud detail evenly across bands and cut the smudged transitions"
R3-jupiter-part2, reviewer_1154, score 5:
- major, image-14.png (jupiter.rise 3296), sunlit left and southern limb in crops F, G, H (also image-13.png sunlit limb, image-15.png crops L, P, Q, and image-16.png crop V top edge): "Jupiter's sunlit edge is a jagged stair-stepped sphere cutoff wrapped in a pasted outer glow halo, and unoccluded background stars shine straight through the bright atmospheric haze and overlap the limb edge itself" Fix named: "supersample and anti-alias the planet limb, replace the additive bloom halo with a physically integrated atmospheric scattering falloff, and mask or attenuate background stars behind both the planet disc and dense limb glare"
- major, image-15.png (jupiter.rise 3296), moon in crop Q upper-right of the Great Red Spot (also image-13.png center and image-14.png crop A right edge): "at native size the moon is visibly pixelated with a raw stair-stepped sunlit left edge, blocky surface blotches, and an airbrushed dark smudge directly beneath its south pole that reads as a fake 2D drop shadow" Fix named: "render the moon at higher supersampled resolution with clean anti-aliased limb edges and crisp surface detail, and remove the misplaced blurry drop-shadow smudge underneath it"
- major, image-15.png (jupiter.rise 3296), Jupiter cloud bands, Great Red Spot, and polar limbs in crops L and Q (also image-13.png globe and image-14.png crops A, G, H): "the globe texture is soft, airbrushed, and under-resolved compared to the crisp 1-2 px background stars, smearing into muddy blur toward both poles while showing pixelated stair-stepping along diagonal creases like the upper-left rim of the Great Red Spot" Fix named: "use a higher-resolution cloud albedo/detail map with proper anisotropic filtering near the poles and limb so cloud filaments stay sharp at 1:1 pixel scale without upscale softness or jagged creases"
- major, image-12.png (jupiter.terrain 3150), near-foreground clouds in crops S, T, U (also image-09.png bottom foreground and image-10.png crops E and H): "the bottom-left foreground clouds in crop S and lower crop H dissolve into a soft, muddy generative smear with mushy orange/grey blobs, while the dark blue storm troughs in crop U and image-10.png crop E mix airbrushed cyan glow patches with isolated pixelated white/blue pinpoint dots on the clouds" Fix named: "render crisp near-field cloud detail (or physically consistent camera depth-of-field) instead of uneven foreground blur, and replace the airbrushed cyan blotches and stray pinpoint specks with coherent volumetric internal cloud lightning"
- minor, image-10.png (jupiter.terrain 3150), diagonal vortex cloud ridges and upper-right limb in crops B, C, E, G, I (also image-09.png and image-11.png crops Q, R): "high-contrast diagonal cloud rims show crunchy oversharpened stair-stepped aliasing at native scale, the upper-right limb has a hard cyan rim line with blobby stars inside the blue atmospheric haze, and the Milky Way dust lanes carry blotchy color noise" Fix named: "reduce post-sharpening/upscale ringing with clean supersampling on diagonal cloud edges, soften the cyan limb line into a smooth atmospheric gradient that dims overlapping stars, and denoise the Milky Way background"
- minor, image-14.png (jupiter.rise 3296), unlit nightside hemisphere and right limb in crop C (also image-15.png crops M and R): "faint noisy blue patches and a dim bluish rim remain slightly visible on Jupiter's unlit nightside with minor dark-tone banding near the terminator" Fix named: "drop unlit nightside surface illumination cleanly to zero while still occluding background stars, and dither the terminator shadow falloff from 16-bit to eliminate dark-side blotches and banding"
"

R3-jupiter-part1, score 5:
- major, image-07.png (jupiter.dive 2997), crops J and K (northern polar region and upper globe, also visible in image-05.png and image-06.png crops A, B, D): "Jupiter's cloud surface softens into heavy directional blur and coarse diagonal streaks across the closer second frame, with stretched and mushy polar texels at native crop scale" Fix named: "use a higher-resolution polar-corrected planetary texture (or cube-sphere/multi-tile mapping) and eliminate surface motion or temporal smearing so cloud filaments stay crisp at close range"
- major, image-06.png (jupiter.dive 2997), crop F lower-right Milky Way cloud (and continuing vertically down the left third of crop W in image-08.png): "a straight vertical tile seam cuts through the Milky Way star cloud where two sky-map edges meet with an abrupt brightness jump" Fix named: "blend or re-project the environment sky map across tile boundaries so no hard vertical mosaic seam appears in the background"
- major, image-02.png (jupiter.hold 2895), crops B, C, F, G, H sky background (and matching sky crops in image-03.png, image-06.png, and image-08.png): "at native size the Milky Way backdrop shows visible pixel blocks, compression chroma noise, and blocky multi-pixel star blobs" Fix named: "replace the low-resolution sky panorama with a higher-resolution uncompressed starfield and dust map (or procedural sub-pixel stars over smooth high-res nebulosity) with proper filtering"
- major, image-02.png (jupiter.hold 2895), crop A moon Io and cast shadow (also image-03.png crop O, image-06.png crop I, and image-07.png crop P): "Io's surface texture is visibly pixelated and blurry in the closer frame, with a stair-stepped silhouette, a dark rim artifact on the upper-right edge, and an aliased cast-shadow edge on Jupiter" Fix named: "increase Io's texture map resolution and sphere subdivision, supersample the moon limb, and soften/anti-alias the cast shadow penumbra on the cloud deck"
- major, image-03.png (jupiter.hold 2895), crop J upper limb (also lower limb in image-04.png crop S and image-08.png crop T, bright left limb in image-02.png crops B/D, and dark right limb in image-02.png crop C and image-06.png crop E): "the bright left atmospheric glow clips to flat white and then disappears abruptly toward both poles, leaving a bare, jagged stair-stepped sphere edge against space and across the Milky Way" Fix named: "extend a smooth, unclipped atmospheric scattering falloff continuously around the lit limb to the poles and apply higher geometric anti-aliasing along both sunlit and dark silhouettes"
- minor, image-02.png (jupiter.hold 2895), crop C unlit night side of Jupiter: "faint ambient light leaks onto the dark side past the terminator, revealing isolated cyan concentric storm rings floating in the shadow" Fix named: "clamp night-side ambient illumination to zero past the terminator falloff so unlit cloud albedo does not leak through"

What main's stop is (read 05:40 to 05:55): the ground is `CloudDeck` of `clouds.ts`, a march on a full screen quad through three height maps (fore, near, mid); the height is already the depth model's (Depth Anything V2 Small, the pinned ONNX of `tools/ml/depth.py`, by `deck/relief.py`: the log range of the canvas above its 120 px mean times `tool.heightGain` 3.3). The colour is the approved picture looked up through the fit camera where the range test says the fit camera sees the point, else the laid back view chart (a ground map with the hidden ground filled), else the plates. So main is not flat by construction; it is flat by size: fore height min -0.198 max 0.144 units, std 0.0489, under a camera 0.98 units up that looks at the storm along a line of slope 0.3. Main's own bake hides 0.028 of the seen ground (NOTES-CODE line 304).

Design of the relief (`relief.ts`, class `ReliefDeck`, the deck's program copied and changed; `body.json` holds every number; `look.ground` picks `plate` or `relief`):
- Height: the deck's height times a gain by place. First try, 06:05: gain 2.2 everywhere. MEASURED: the bake hides 0.146 of the seen ground and marks 0.146 steep; at 3200 thin bright slivers and smeared fill stand along the silhouettes of the near towers and far ridges (picture `out/work/view/rb/relief/d3200.png` of that state, crop `/tmp/c4.jpg`), worse than main. Cause: far out the sight line lies flat (slope 0.3 at the storm, less beyond), a taller top hides ground the picture never shows and the fill has to invent it. Second try, 06:15: the gain follows the sight line, `gain` 3.0 inside 0.6 units of the fit camera's foot, 1 past 2.8 units.
- My own depth run (`out/work/jupiter/body/canvas-depth.raw`, 2816 x 1584, 19.84 s on this CPU) is not used: its 120 to 400 px band shows no storm structure; what the model knows of the storm lies under 120 px and main's height holds that already.
- A pit for the storm's eye as a large form is NOT in the height. Reason, by geometry: the fit camera looks at the storm along slope 0.3; a pit whose near wall falls away steeper than that is hidden from the picture, and one near that slope stretches a few picture pixels over the whole wall. A bowl of 0.14 units is the most that keeps the near wall at 0.4 of the sight line's slope. Not built.
- Flow: no ring turns. One law for the storm (angle = bounded time law times a share by radius, 0 at the centre, full between 1.1 and 1.6 units, 0 at 5), built from main's own form (the first seam's turn set to minus the storm's turn) so the globe's program, which shares the uniforms, moves the same matter. Band shear only in my program, taken off above 2 to 10 units of camera height. Backward look up, exact, no reset.
- Light: ratio of the light now over the picture's light, from the height's normal and a marched soft shadow; exactly 1 with the Sun in its place and no turn.
- Haze: its shadows are read in the deck's own height scale (the ground's height over its gain), so 3150 keeps main's haze. Before that the taller ground moved the haze shadow behind the storm and the low band at the draft tier read 3.4354 against main's 3.2099 (my re-implementation of bands.py, `/tmp/bands.py`).
- Deep glow: first form went black on slivers that the fit camera sees edge on (the change was subtracted without a floor); now weighted by the facing to the fit camera and floored.

### 06:24 to 06:35 UTC: the guard tried and dropped; the black cracks are rays that ran out of steps

- Guard (the range picture eroded by 2 texels beside every jump of more than 0.04): bake printed "guard texels 107919 share 0.0242", hidden of seen went from 0.155 to 0.212. MEASURED at the draft tier, 3150: low16 3.6450 against main's 3.2099, plain 6.8710 against 6.3207, the top luminance band 0.966 against main's 0.989. SEEN: columns of smear under the horizon (the range jumps by more than 0.04 from texel to texel there, so the far ground was held hidden and the fill showed), and the cracks at 3200 no thinner. Dropped; `guard` is out of body.py and body.json.
- The cracks at 3200 (`out/work/view/rb/r2/d3200.png`, x 430 to 450, y 972 to 985) read 1 1 1 to 3 2 2: blacker than anything the picture holds. They are no fill. Cause: the march steps at least 0.75 of a pixel's foot, 400 such steps carry a ray only 1.41 times its range (e to the 400 x 0.75 / 864), and a ray that runs close over a taller top spends them and falls through to the sky's black. Pixels of the lower half of 3200 with a sum under 6: relief 121, main 23.
- Cure: `march[3]` 1200 steps; the slope the step law counts on is `march[0]` times the gain of the place (the deck's 4.2 holds for the deck's height only); a ray that spends its steps without leaving the shell counts as landed where it is. The fit camera's range picture marches with slope 12.6 (4.2 times gain 3.0).
- `flow.bands.unitsPerSecond` 0.02 to 0.012 (0.02 gave about 26 px a second of drift in the near field with the camera held, by the law's own numbers).
- Stills in one session: `npx tsx assets-src/jupiter/deck/frames.ts --frames 3200,3150,3100 --name NAME --tier draft` (writes `out/work/jupiter/frames/NAME/fNNNNNN.png`).

### 06:35 to 06:50 UTC: the cracks are the fill's detail, not the march; the gain by place was the wrong way round

The `layers` view (`npm run -s still -- --frame 3200 --tier draft --diag layers --out /tmp/l3200.png`) reads blue 255 on every crack (the image field fully trusted) and red 13 to 45 of 255 (the steps far from spent). So the black is in the view chart itself: `deck/view.py` `carry_fill` adds the mirrored fine structure as a difference and clamps at zero; in the wide gaps of a taller ground it went below zero. The chart held 76321 texels with a sum under 6 before and 19846 after.

Reason by numbers: the strip that comes into view is about focal x move x step of height / (eye height x range): at range 1 and a move of 0.4 units, 345 px per unit of height step, so 0.1 units at gain 3.0 opens about 104 px; at the storm (range 3.4) the same step opens about 10 px at gain 1. I had given the near field the gain. Turned round: `gain` 1.0 inside 1.2 units of the foot, `farGain` 2.0 from 3.0 to 5.5 units, 1 past 8 (the far ridges keep the sky line of the picture).

- The cure as designed, "variant G", in scratch /tmp/gsrc (tsc exit 0, 4 files, 25 insertions, 10 deletions against the cut): relief.ts exports BODY_GLSL; globe.ts uses it in place of STORED_GLSL, so its place program marches stored x tall(g) with a gain of its own (uniform uPlaceTall of the relief's uniforms, passed as the globe's uTall) and steps by fall + uMarch.x x tall(at); set.ts sets that gain to tallNow in the rise and 0 everywhere else, and placeBody() gives the globe the body's shell and march while the gain is over 0, the deck's otherwise; tallNow(seconds, height) reads body.height.fadeHeights in the dive (seconds under 0) and the new body.handover.riseTallHeights [0.006, 0.09] in the rise. With the gain 0, tall is exactly 1.0 and the globe's numbers are the deck's, so the dive, the hold, the surface view and the rise from 3272 on should keep their bytes: to be proved by cmp, not assumed. Expected to change: 3241 to 3272 where the globe shows beside the layer, and the body from 3253 + 1/2 to 3272.

Main's ground is deck.ts, which has no trust by smear; I did not read how main bounds its image there and ran no variants on main.
- In the shader (relief.ts, the ground's colour): `col = mix(ground * shade * through + added, seen.rgb * fine * shade, seen.a)`; `through` and `added` come from the low air's table by the sine of the line of sight at the ground, then `thin = uAirLift` (by the eye's height, one number for the whole frame) mixes to the look from above (`uAboveGain`, `uAboveAir` by the same sine); `toned` (by the eye's height, one number) multiplies the plates by `toneAt(g) * calmAt(g)`. At the far edge the sine is near 0: under the low air the plates are all but hidden (dark blue haze), under the look from above they are bright cream to the limb. That is where the two looks differ most, and both veils change for the whole frame at once.

### 13:52 UTC, the far strip: the picture leaves it between 3250 and 3258; builds A1 and A2 tested; where the reader's figure comes from

- Reader's pattern, read from tools/img/smooth.py (`pattern`, `readings`): detail between two widths (2 and 10 px on the picture reduced to 480 wide), divided by local contrast, judged only where both frames have contrast over the floor 1; the row's figure is the 99th percentile. A pair is read twice: onward (the first frame against the later one carried back), and back (the later frame against the first one carried onto it) only on the pixels where the first frame is enlarged in the later one. A veil that only lifts level counts little; a swap of detail counts fully.
- Chain 30 (13:20:30 to 13:26:53, 0 status lines after): a map of the approved picture's weight (`seen.a`, working tree only, /tmp/vdiag.py; /tmp/sheet-vdiag.jpg). The picture fills the frame to the limb at 3244 and 3250, shrinks from the edges at 3254, and is a patch round the storm at 3258, 3262, 3266. So on the pair 3250 and 3258 the far strip is the picture, then the plates. The rule: `seen.a = edge * (1 - trust[2]) * (1 - smoothstep(trust[0], trust[1], smear / uFrame))`, deck.json look.trust [1.0, 2.5, 0, 0].
- Read from the code: at the far strip (over 20 units out) neither the tone map (a box 32 units wide, known [5, 7], fade [5, 9]) nor the haze bands act. What acts there: the air table by the sine, the look from above by `thin`, the deep glow, and the picture.

- The build's final form (/tmp/build_b.py, patch /tmp/air-b.patch): the name's own loop for the air table is not touched. A helper `airAt(sine, line, through, added)` reads the two tables with `textureLod` only. After the name's `col = mix(...)`: `lowered = max(above(E) - uAirHeld.x, 0)`, `carried = lowered > 0 ? seen.a * uAirHeld.z : 0`; where `carried` is over 0 the pixel reads the air now and the air along the line of sight from the eye lowered to the ceiling, and the picture's share of the pixel moves by `lit * airNow - was` (never under `uAirHeld.y` times the picture); `liftLow` and `liftClear` carry the same into `shallow` and `aloft`. The float tables have linear filters and no mip levels (src/core/sets.ts `floatTexture`), so `textureLod` at level 0 reads what `texture` reads.
- Shader audit (`python3 tools/shaderaudit.py`): exit 0, 49 lines, 10 of them in src/stops/jupiter; the head's audit (from `git archive HEAD`) has the same 10 but for line numbers. `npm run build` exit 0.
